# Prevention register — our own bugs, and the artefact that stops each class

<!--
Navigation
----------
What it is:   The register of bugs found in our own work: one row per defect, with its class,
              when it first bit us, and the artefact that fails if the class comes back.
What it does: Holds STANDARD §7 as a record: a row is closed only by an executable artefact
              that resolves (test, vitest, spec or ci), and a pending row names the gap that
              will close it; scripts/dod_check.py checks every row and renders the counts and
              the pending rows, each with its claim tag, into docs/dod/GAP-ANALYSIS.md.
How:          The `## Register` table (8 cells a row) and the `## Gaps` lines the pending rows
              cite; a gap line no pending row cites is refused, and deleted when its row closes.
Layer:        docs — docs/ARCHITECTURE.md#44-outer-layers
ADRs:         docs/adr/0020-a-bug-is-closed-by-prevention.md (the product's own loop, the same rule)
Works with:   docs/dod/STANDARD.md (§7, the rule), scripts/dod_check.py (the checker),
              docs/dod/GAP-ANALYSIS.md (where the counts land), .github/pull_request_template.md
              (asks every bug fix for its row), docs/dod/PLAN.md (the waves that close the
              pending rows)
Tested by:    tests/test_dod_check.py (the register's refusals)
Touch when:   a gate, a test, a reviewer or the live stack finds a bug in our work (add the row
              with the fix), or a pending row's gap closes (close the row, delete its gap line).
-->

**Why.** The product's learning loop has one rule, set by the operator on 2026-09-25: *learn
from a bug, then go back and change the process or the context so it cannot happen again.*
We hold ourselves to the same rule. Every bug we hit while building the product is a row
here, with the class it belongs to, when it first bit us, and the artefact that now stops the
class — an artefact that **fails** if the class comes back.

**The rule** (`docs/dod/STANDARD.md` §7): a defect is closed only with the artefact that fails
if its class recurs. `scripts/dod_check.py --check` (CI job `dod`) enforces it on this file:

- every artefact reference resolves, in the typed forms of STANDARD §3 (`test:`, `vitest:`,
  `spec:`, `ci:`, `code:` …);
- a `closed` row cites at least one reference that can fail (`test:`, `vitest:`, `spec:` or
  `ci:`) — a `code:` reference proves a thing exists, not that it works;
- `advisory` text never closes a row: prose cannot fail when the class recurs, so an advisory
  fix stays `pending` with a gap toward a gate;
- a `pending` row names its gap (`G-7nn` below, with the owner layer) or a backlog id.

**The levels**, strongest first — pick the strongest the class admits:
`construction` (the wrong thing cannot be built) › `gate` (a deterministic check refuses it) ›
`mistake-proofing` (the input makes the mistake hard) › `advisory` (a sentence someone must
read — the fallback, never the first choice).

The pull-request template (`.github/pull_request_template.md`) asks every change that fixes a
bug for its row here.

**Numbering.** The register began on the value branch (`feat/value`), whose rows are
`P-001` onwards. The posture branch (`feat/posture`, PR #56, and its review fixes in PR #61)
numbered its rows from `P-101`, so the two registers joined without renumbering **[measured — n = 2 registers; method: the ids of both registers read at the merge; apparatus n/a, a property of the product's own code, not a graded row]**. The
north-star waves (`feat/ns1`) numbered theirs from `P-051` on their own branch; when that
branch took `main` in, they were renumbered in order from `P-118`, after the posture rows,
and every reference on the branch moved with them. Wave 2 (`feat/ns2`) numbered its rows from `P-227` while `feat/ns1`'s review fixes took `P-227` to `P-234`; when `feat/ns1` was merged in, Wave 2's rows moved up by eight, to `P-235` onwards, with every reference (P-334).

**Tags.** Each row's finding carries its claim tag (`docs/EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS.md` §1), and a
`[measured]` one carries its n, its method and its apparatus version. The claims gate skips
tables, so `tests/test_prevention_register.py` reads the rows with the gate's own check and
fails on a row that owes one (P-109). A pending row reaches the gap analysis with its tag:
`scripts/dod_check.py` carries it after the finding and refuses a pending row with none, and
the same test file fails on a projected row that arrives without one (P-433).

The balance rests on per-file seconds measured once (`scripts/ci_test_weights.json`): a shard that grows is failed by its own guard at 24 of its 30 minutes, and the fix is `ci_test_shards.py weights` over one run's reports, or a larger N |
| P-054 | A test passed only because an earlier test had warmed a shared cache: Maven setup warmed the local repository with `mvn test -DskipTests` and its docstring said that warmed surefire, but `-DskipTests` stops surefire before it resolves its test-framework provider (`surefire-junit-platform` and the `junit-platform-launcher` aligned with the project's JUnit), so the first offline `mvn -o test` on a cold local repository failed, and `environment_ready` (the same goal offline) read ready. The regression test runs setup, readiness and the offline run against an EMPTY local repository private to the test, so neither the host's warm cache nor an earlier test can hide the class again, and a repository launcher listener proves setup ran no test code, then that an in-process POM is refused with nothing run. Sharding is the detector for the class in general: a test that leans on another test's side effect goes red when a shard separates them |

| P-440 | The credential routes changed the token on disk before the audited commit took the `events` write lock: `PUT` wrote the new value and `DELETE` removed it, then `commit_audited` recorded the event. When the lock was refused (SQLite's `database is locked` under another writer) or the commit failed, the request answered 500 with the token already replaced or gone and no `settings.secret_set` / `settings.secret_deleted` event, breaking EI-8's rule that every credential change is an event. The store and the removal now run inside the audited write, after the lock, so a refused lock changes nothing (P-443 then moved the file operation after the event as well); a removal re-run after a lost `seq` race reads whether the token existed on the first run only. A file cannot join the transaction, so a crash, or a commit that fails after the file write, can still leave a change unrecorded [hypothesis — not measured; the window is the commit after the write] | side-effect-outside-audited-write | CodeRabbit's review of #63 (5338916728), 2026-09-28 [measured — n = 2 of 2 cases failing on the pre-fix branch (the Claude Code token and the tracker token were both gone after a `DELETE` whose lock was refused), and the source ratchet found 3 credential writes outside the audited write, method: the new lock-refusal test and ratchet run against 841861a7; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own code, not a graded row] | `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_credential_is_not_changed_when_its_event_cannot_be_written` · `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_every_credential_change_happens_inside_its_audited_write` · `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_removal_retried_after_a_lost_race_still_records_that_the_token_existed` | construction | closed | |
| P-441 | DEPLOYMENT §3.3's grants gave the application role `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on `alembic_version`, though only the owner migrates, so the API or the worker could rewrite the schema's recorded version and hide an unapplied migration; and they covered only the tables that existed, so a table a later release adds had no grant at all. The split-role tests typed their own grants, which matched the guide by hand and so could not catch it. The tests now run the guide's SQL block as written; the guide grants `alembic_version` `SELECT` only and adds default grants (`SELECT, INSERT` on later tables, `USAGE, SELECT` on later sequences), and a text check on every run requires each model table to be granted once, the append-only ones `SELECT, INSERT` only | guide-grant-drifts-from-proof | CodeRabbit's review of #63 (5338916728), 2026-09-28 [measured — n = 1 split-role run of the guide's grants on PostgreSQL 16.15, where the application role's `UPDATE alembic_version` succeeded, method: the extended split-role test run against 841861a7; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own code, not a graded row] | `test:tests/test_store_db.py::test_an_application_role_that_does_not_own_the_tables_cannot_remove_the_protection` · `test:tests/test_store_db.py::test_the_guides_grants_name_every_table_and_only_the_owner_writes_the_version` | gate | closed | |

| P-443 | P-440's fix put the credential file operation inside the audited write but still ahead of the event: `_stored` wrote the file and `_removed` removed it, and only then did `_record_secret_change` read the trace's last `seq` and add the event. A database error in that step that is not a lost race (so `commit_audited` does not retry it) answered 500 with the token already replaced or gone and no `settings.secret_*` event, the gap EI-8 and P-440 close, and P-440's "just before the commit" overstated the fix. Inside the audited write the event is now recorded and flushed first, with the fingerprint worked out from the cleaned value (`SecretsFile.fingerprint_of`, writing nothing), and the file operation is the last step, so only the commit follows it; a race lost at that commit re-runs the write after the file went, and whether a removed token existed is still read on the first run only | side-effect-outside-audited-write | The adversarial check of the P-440 answer on #63 (thread 4122374824), 2026-09-28 [measured — n = 3 of 3 checks failing on 5034e2cc: the event-insert failure left the Claude Code token and the tracker token both gone after a `DELETE` answered 500, and the order ratchet found 2 file operations ahead of the event (`_stored`, `_removed`); method: the new tests run against 5034e2cc, and the commit-race test failing when the first-run guard is removed; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own code, not a graded row] | `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_credential_is_not_changed_when_recording_its_event_fails` · `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_credential_file_operation_is_the_last_step_before_the_commit` · `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_removal_whose_commit_lost_the_race_still_records_that_the_token_existed` · `test:tests/test_server_routes_admin_secrets.py::test_a_stored_token_s_event_carries_the_fingerprint_it_was_stored_with` | construction | closed | |

| P-675 | #56 committed its review evidence (`docs/reviews/2026-09-25-sealed-posture/`) with dependency-store keys — `dep_` + a sha256 hex digest, made by `crb.core.provision.bundle_key` in the shape `crb.core.deps.KEY_RE` defines — under `"key"` fields, and gitleaks' default `generic-api-key` rule read each as an API key. Main's `security (gitleaks + pip-audit)` job went red on 406ff747's push and nothing surfaced it; a later push to main scans only its own commits, so the findings came back only where a scan range reached 406ff747 — and every pull request whose range does fails that required check (PR #75 at db5a6150). `.gitleaks.toml` now allowlists the key's exact shape for that one rule, anchored and matched against the captured secret, so a credential glued to a key is still a finding. The full-history scan then found the same misreading of ADR-0025's HMAC label `crb.ledger.anchor.v1` (#69's push 435d2a49), a fixed domain-separation string the ADR publishes by design; it is allowlisted as that one literal label, anchored, for that one rule. A test holds each allowlist regex to the product's own definition, string for string — `KEY_RE`'s pattern (with `KEY_RE` flag-free), and `^` + the escaped label + `$`, the label read from ADR-0025 because no code constant defines it yet — so a change of key format or label, or a loosened regex, fails the build; a test reads the config the way gitleaks does, every key case-insensitively (two spellings of one key are refused), and pins all of it — every allowlist entry by its scope and every key but its description, `[extend]` as gitleaks' own rules whole (no `disabledRules`, no `path`), and no rule of our own — so a widened, loosened, added or removed entry, a rule-level allowlist however its keys are spelled, a disabled default rule or a custom rule fails the build until that inventory changes with a stated reason (an inventory that read keys exactly let `[[rules.AllowLists]]` hide a planted token that gitleaks 8.30.1 honoured); and a test keeps the config's doctrine that no allowlisted path reaches docs/, src/ or deploy/ | public-value-read-as-secret | PR #75's CI run 37840877574 (job 113529625649) on 2026-10-08, and main's push run 36280792423 of 406ff747 (job 108512081346) on 2026-09-26 23:52 UTC [measured — n = 27 findings in each run, all `generic-api-key` in commit 406ff747 (18 in `qualifications.jsonl`, 6 in `proof.json`, 2 in `mine-events.jsonl`, 1 in `deps-verify-after-refetch.json`), method: the jobs' log lines `RuleID:`, `File:` and `Commit:`, and gitleaks 8.30.1 run locally with `--redact` on the PR job's own scan range (27 before the allowlist, 0 after) while a planted credential under docs/ is still reported; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own CI] | `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_allowlist_accepts_exactly_what_key_re_accepts` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_a_real_key_matches_the_products_own_shape` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_dep_key_allowlist_is_scoped_to_one_rule_and_the_secret_alone` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_every_secret_target_regex_passes_a_whole_secret_or_nothing` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_evidence_that_tripped_the_rule_is_passed` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_no_allowlisted_path_reaches_docs_src_or_deploy` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_path_check_refuses_a_planted_path` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_adr_0025_anchor_label_is_passed_alone` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_allowlist_inventory_is_pinned` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_inventory_refuses_a_planted_change` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_inventory_ignores_descriptions` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_rest_of_the_config_is_pinned` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_config_refuses_a_planted_text_change` · `test:tests/test_gitleaks_config.py::test_the_config_reads_keys_as_gitleaks_does` · `test:tests/test_ci_gitleaks.py::test_the_scan_is_redacted_reads_the_config_and_reports_its_verdict` · `ci:security` | gate | closed | nothing alarms when a push to main turns the security job red: 406ff747's went red on 2026-09-26 and surfaced only when PR #75 inherited it 11 days and 21 hours later, and #69's push 435d2a49 went red the same way on 2026-10-08 (generic-api-key on ADR-0025's HMAC label `crb.ledger.anchor.v1`) [measured — n = 2 red security jobs on main pushes (runs 36280792423 and 37750661664), method: the runs' job conclusions and their `RuleID:`/`File:` log lines; | P-676 | Wave 6's merge of `feat/ns4` put Wave 4's builder-login check in `submit_refusals`, which reads `settings.builder.login_ttl_s` and `.claude_binary` for every kind before the check looks at the kind, while Wave 6's stage chain handed the gate a `SimpleNamespace` stand-in for the API's settings with only `home` and `factory`: every chained stage raised `AttributeError: 'types.SimpleNamespace' object has no attribute 'builder'`, caught and logged, so with `auto_stages` on no chained stage was ever queued. P-672's class came back because `settings: Any` keeps mypy blind. A typed Protocol catches it only with a typed stand-in — mypy lets a `SimpleNamespace` satisfy any Protocol (typeshed's `__getattribute__` returns `Any`) — and a static contract would be the union of every branch's reads, which would force a made-up `sandbox` onto the chain, whose bodies never reach the branch that reads it; so a runtime read-recording test was chosen. The stand-ins are named methods: `Worker._chain_gate_settings`, for the pair the one seam `Worker._chain_request` makes and the chain submits, and `Worker._claim_check_settings`, which `_credential_gone` hands `credential_refusal` when the worker claims a run. Tests record every attribute path read off the API's own `Settings` — the gate's over exactly that pair, `params.chained_from` and the chain's actor set, the credential check's for a run of every kind the worker can claim — and fail when one does not resolve on the stand-in | worker-api-settings-drift | the merge of `feat/ns4` into `feat/ns6` (6a324ef0), 2026-10-08, fixed in 30c08b7b [measured — n = 8 tests, method: tests/test_worker_chain.py re-run in a worktree at 9afc6b72, the commit before the fix: 4 failed, 4 passed, each failure the `AttributeError` on `builder`; 8 passed at db5a6150; the merge's parents read: cea278ca had the two-field stand-in and no login read, 7b1cf247 the login read and no chain; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own code, not a graded row] | `test:tests/test_worker_chain.py::test_the_chain_gate_settings_carry_every_field_the_submit_gate_reads` · `test:tests/test_worker_chain.py::test_the_gate_settings_check_reports_a_stand_in_without_builder` · `test:tests/test_worker_chain.py::test_the_claim_check_settings_carry_every_field_the_credential_check_reads` · `test:tests/test_worker_chain.py::test_the_claim_check_reports_a_stand_in_without_a_field_it_reads` · `test:tests/test_worker_chain.py::test_a_passing_probe_queues_the_mine_when_the_switch_is_on` · `test:tests/test_worker.py::test_an_s1_run_on_the_deployments_test_author_whose_credential_went_stops_at_claim` | gate | closed |  |

| P-751 | `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` ran apt-get as root with nothing bounding it: when the runners' Azure Ubuntu mirror stopped answering, apt printed its last fetch line and waited, silently, until the job's own `timeout-minutes` cancelled it, 20 to 40 minutes later. Six required browser jobs on pull requests #58, #66 and #69 were lost this way in an hour, and a rerun met the same mirror. Later that evening the mirror was slow, not dead: apt prints nothing while a package downloads, so healthy attempts went quiet for minutes, and the first limits (3 attempts, 180 s of silence, 600 s in all) would have cut installs that were going to finish. ## Gaps
- **G-738** — the Decisions inbox derives "sign-off due" from the capability map's verification tier (`apply_signoffs`, `covers_reading`), while the entry gate reads `Standard.signed` (`signed_by`: an exact arm, class-set version and reading, and an apparatus stamp); the two rules differ, so a cell can read earned on the map and the inbox while the gate stops its tickets `unsigned_cell` and no row asks anyone to sign it (P-411) · make `decision_rows` (src/crb/server/decisions.py) and the map's tier read the entry gate's `standard_for`, and add a test in which the map is earned but the standard is unsigned and the inbox must show a `signoff_due` row · server
- **G-791** — the Maven and Cargo runners still inherit the worker's `PATH` on the host (P-448) · give `MavenRunner` and `CargoRunner` a `declared_tools` list (Maven: `mvn` or `./mvnw`, `java`, `git` and the basics its launcher script calls; Cargo: `cargo`, `rustc`, `rustdoc`, the linker `cc` and `ld`, with `CARGO_HOME` and `RUSTUP_HOME` passed through), with the leak fixture of `tests/test_runners_toolenv.py` repeated per runner on a warm cache · server
- **G-792** — a Maven belt run can stop at the first failing module and never observe the modules after it (P-449) · reproduce it on a two-module fixture whose first module holds a baseline failure, then run the belt scope with `-fae` and flag any module the reactor summary reports as failed or skipped without a surefire report as a `parse_error`, as the Go parser now does for a package that names no test · server
- **G-793** — the oracle controls' compile checks and belt 5's lint plan still inherit the worker's `PATH` on the host (P-455) · run `crb.oracle.controls`' compile commands and `crb.core.lint.run_plan`'s commands through the runner's `declare`, with the leak fixture of `tests/test_runners_toolenv.py` repeated for a control and a lint plan · server
- **G-794** — the node runners' parsers may read a test process that exits part-way as a verdict (P-454) · reproduce `process.exit(1)` part-way through a jest, vitest, mocha and `node --test` run beside a baselined failure, then make the run unattributed when the reporter's totals are missing or short, as the Go and pytest parsers now do · server
- **G-938** — the run's spend cap is a guard, not a guarantee (ADR-0030 §3): an attempt with no cost cap of its own — which is how the Measure and Factory pages submit — and a test author's call are counted at the dearest so far, nothing before the first, so a run can pass its cap by up to one attempt or item before it stops `failed` and says so; and an attempt with a cost cap of its own can pass that cap by the model call that reached it · bound each model call before it is made — refuse a call whose worst case (its prompt's input tokens, all at the uncached input price, plus its output-token limit at the output price) would not fit what is left of the run, recorded so the row's experiment and `budget_tier` are unchanged — then flip `factory.actions.8` · server
- **G-981** — a `ci:` reference resolves when the job exists, never when it has passed, so a criterion can be set to met on a CI job that has never run green (P-266) · have `scripts/dod_check.py` refuse a met criterion whose `ci:` reference is not beside a `measured:` reference that names a green run of that job · docs
- **G-939** — a criterion that quotes page copy ("…") is not checked against the page, so a change to the copy leaves the criterion describing a page that no longer exists (P-267) · have `scripts/dod_check.py` find each quoted string in a page or journey criterion under `ui/src/` and refuse a `met` or `partial` row whose quote no screen or hint carries · docs
- **G-930** — the required-check list is a repository setting that no gate re-reads: `scripts/check_branch_protection.py` fails when it and the jobs of the pull-request workflows disagree, and the daily `branch-protection` workflow runs it, but no token that may read the setting is provisioned, so the workflow is red and a check an administrator drops from the list would merge unseen; the list itself is whole — an administrator required the `fresh-clone` and `commit-subjects` checks on 2026-10-07, and the saved reading parks no job under `awaiting_protection` **[measured — n = 1 reading of main's branch protection; method: `data/branch-protection-2026-10-07/` (`required_status_checks.json` against `workflow_jobs.json`) read by `scripts/check_branch_protection.py`; apparatus n/a, a repository-setting reading, not a graded row]** · an administrator adds a fine-grained token with Administration: read as the repository secret `BRANCH_PROTECTION_TOKEN` (docs/DEPLOYMENT.md §3.4), then cite the `branch-protection` workflow's first green run · deploy
- **G-989** — page copy that says what the API does not serve is not tied to the API's shape, so it outlives the field that makes it false (P-133) · a help-copy ratchet that reads each "does not yet serve" / "means only" / "not recorded" sentence in `ui/src/help/` against a declared field list and fails when the field exists · ui
- **G-990** — the flat `cost_usd_mean` / `latency_s_mean` on each cell follow the map's own filters, so a map read with `posture=all` or `apparatus=all` averages them across classes or versions; `pareto_frontier`, `best_config` and the forecast's `_axis` price read the same pooled rows (the forecast loads every apparatus version and posture class of one arm) (P-134) · make a cell's `cost_known` / `latency_known` false when its economics fold is pooled, so the frontier and the best config leave it out, and price the forecast from the fold over one apparatus version and posture class, with a test that mixes a sealed and a host row in one config · server
- **G-991** — `GET /value` sums spend and counts working changes over every posture class: the route takes no posture parameter and `crb.core.value` reads no posture label, so a sealed row and a host row pool in one rate per pound (P-135) · filter the scorecard's rows by posture class as `rows_for_posture` does for the map (the deployment's class by default, `posture=` on request), and refuse a reduction over two classes, with a route test that mixes a sealed and a host row · server
- **G-994** — nothing checks that a criterion flipped to met kept its words, or that a changed file's header still says what the file does (P-172) · make `scripts/dod_check.py` compare each row that becomes met against the base branch and refuse a changed criterion text unless the change names it in a `reworded:` line of the gap register, and have the header check flag a file whose diff is large while its What it does line is unchanged · docs
- **G-995** — a wave's parallel streams each fix a shared defect class their own way, and nothing names one owner per class (P-190) · make the wave plan name an owner for each shared class it expects (axe settling, served tree, focus return), and have `scripts/dod_check.py` refuse two register rows opened in one wave that share a class slug unless one cites the other · docs
- **G-998** — a `[measured]` tag can name a source the repository does not carry, and nothing refuses it (P-192) · extend `scripts/claims_check.py` to `docs/PREVENTION.md` and `docs/DECISION-LOG.md` and require each `[measured]` method to name a path in the repository or a pinned commit · docs
- **G-996** — nothing checks that a test cited as a closed gate can fail, except for the go-live stream's own behaviours: a source grep passed with the behaviour it guarded switched off (P-178), and stream P's evidence did the same five times (P-359, now a gate in `tests/test_golive_evidence_mutations.py` for those behaviours alone) · a mutation pass over the evidence of every other `closed` row and `met` criterion — switch each named behaviour off and require at least one cited test to fail — run on a schedule, its survivors listed as findings; the go-live file's pattern (copy the source, one exact substitution, run the cited tests against the copy, a control run beside them) is the one to generalise · docs
- **G-709** — the Helm chart and the compose file give the migration job, the API and the worker one `CRB_DATABASE_URL`, so on PostgreSQL the running application owns the append-only tables and could disable their triggers (P-210) [measured — n = 1 run, method: `tests/test_store_db.py::test_a_trigger_missing_on_a_split_role_store_is_restored_by_the_owner_not_the_application` on a local PostgreSQL 16.15, where the role that ran `crb migrate` (on a single-role deployment, the application) drops `grades_no_delete` and the probe reads `down`; apparatus n/a, a finding about the product's own code, not a graded row]; the split is documented and tested but is an operator's manual procedure · give the migration job its own owner URL (`CRB_MIGRATE_DATABASE_URL`, falling back to `CRB_DATABASE_URL`) in the chart and compose, and a `crb doctor` line that says `warn` when the application role owns the ledger tables · deploy
- **G-935** — the promise rule holds only the capabilities someone registers in `PROMISES` in `scripts/claims_check.py` (P-235), so a present-tense sentence about any other unbuilt capability on a public page still needs a reader to catch it · have `scripts/dod_check.py` refuse an unmet criterion whose words name a public page (README, a guide or EVIDENCE-AND-CLAIMS) unless `PROMISES` registers a promise for it, so each such criterion brings its promise with it · docs
- **G-937** — the claims gate checks that a count carries its tag, never that the count is true, so a count of a list in the code (probes, actions, tests, verbs) stated on a guide can still drift after it is tagged (P-239) **[measured — n = 1 gate; method: `scripts/claims_check.py`'s own statement of what it does not check (that a tag is present, never that its figure is sound), read against its checks; apparatus n/a, a property of the product's own code, not a graded row]** · for each such count, either state none or bind it to a test that re-derives it from the code, as `tests/test_health_probe_docs.py` does for `/health`'s probes · docs
- **G-766** — the gates an integration reports green are run in a shared developer environment that nothing compares with `uv.lock`, so a result can come from versions CI never installs (P-331) · rebuild the shared environment with `uv sync --locked` and CI's extras, and have an integration run the `fresh-clone` job's gates script from the lock before it reports green · deploy
- **G-767** — a wave's plan is not compared with the gaps still open, so a wave can read as done while gaps its rows name are open (P-332) · have `scripts/dod_check.py` refuse a wave section that says it is integrated while its table names an open gap id that its carried-forward list does not · docs
- **G-768** — an accepted ADR's decisions are not compared with the code, so a decision can read as made while nothing builds it (P-333) · a test that reads each accepted ADR's backticked `crb.` symbols and event names and refuses one the code does not carry unless the ADR marks it deferred · docs
- **G-769** — an integration branch is not checked against the current head of the branch it stacks on, so it can be cut from a stale head and reuse the ids the base took since (P-334) · have the integration check `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/<base> HEAD` before it reports green, and number new ids only after reading the base's highest · docs
- **G-732** — the failed-read ratchet (P-369) stops a new read whose fallback could stand in for a failure only when no sibling answers the same query elsewhere in the file: it counts a query answered once its `isError` appears anywhere in the source, so a second row fed by the same read can still show its fallback when the read fails (P-398); only a placeholder shown straight off a missing read (`!q.data ? '…'`) is checked read by read, and a read bound to a local is followed. The eighteen reads it found when it was built are listed in `NOT_YET_READ` in `ui/src/test/failed-reads.test.ts` and not yet read one by one: among them the repository picker (a failed read lists no repository), the Posture page's version (a failed read shows `…` for ever), the Measure page's posture row (`health`), the sign-off page's map and evidence, and the Factory page's map · read each: give it a failure branch (an `isError` read, a `QueryBoundary`, `failedRead`) or show its fallback is honest, and take it off the list, which only shrinks; and make the ratchet answer each read where it is shown, not once per file · ui
- **G-739** — eighteen screens still disable a control while its request runs (`disabled={x.isPending}`), which drops focus to `<body>` for a keyboard user (P-396); the ratchet refuses a new one and lists these in `NOT_YET_MOVED` in `ui/src/test/pending-focus.test.ts` · move each to `pending={x.isPending}` (`Button`, or a GOV.UK button) with its outcome or refusal focused, and take it off the list, which only shrinks · ui
- **G-720** — a read-then-insert under a unique key with two writers is caught only where a test races it (the decisions clock, P-357); nothing finds the next one · a ratchet test that lists every ORM insert into a table with a unique constraint and refuses one that is neither an `ON CONFLICT` statement nor under a documented single writer · server
- **G-112** — a sealed set sealed again under a key it held before (after quarantine, G-966) lands at the same store path, and a daemon reading the store through a file share (colima's virtiofs, likely Docker Desktop's too) can serve the old directory there for about a second, so a container started in that window mounts the quarantined set (P-674); Linux daemons bind the host's inode and do not · seal each set to a path no earlier seal used (a per-seal directory under the key), so a stale lookup can only miss, and have `bundle_mount_args` bind that path · server
